# Backlog — the default home for unsprinted task briefs

**This is not a sprint.** It is the standing board for work that has been scoped into a brief but not
yet scheduled into one. A brief written with no sprint named lands here, and stays here until someone
pulls it into a sprint.

**⚠️ The filename is deliberately `backlog.md`, NOT `sprint-backlog.md`.** `/fkit-status` resolves the
active sprint by each plan's **identity**, with **no pattern on the filename**: this board's identity
is `Backlog`, which is **never eligible**, so the default status run ignores it no matter what the
file is called
([ADR-041 §§2–3](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-041-the-active-sprint-is-selected-by-resolved-identity-not-by-filename-glob.md)).
**That exclusion is stronger than the filename rule it replaced** — renaming this file could not make
it the active sprint. The name is kept for a different reason: its href is written into every
`➡️ Moved to [Backlog](backlog.md)` marker in the repo. **Do not "normalize" this name.**

## How work moves on and off this board

- **On:** `/fkit-task-brief` with no sprint named files the brief with `## Sprint: Backlog` and adds a
  row here (creating this file if it is absent).
- **Off:** when the **producer** pulls a task into a sprint, **three** edits, all mandatory: add the
  row to that sprint plan; flip the row here to `➡️ Moved to [Sprint N](sprint-N.md) — priority M`
  (the canonical marker — `M` is the priority the task gets in Sprint N, and the `— priority M`
  component is **not** optional here **when that sprint board is ranked**); **and update the brief's
  own `## Sprint` to `Sprint N`.**
  The row here is **not deleted** — a deleted row loses the pointer to where the work went.
  > **⚠️ Skip the brief-side update and the row never goes away.** Drift rule 2 compares the `Moved`
  > target against the brief's `## Sprint`; a mismatch is `drift disagreement`, and a drifted row
  > always renders. Every pulled task would leave a permanent drifted row on this board.
  >
  > **⚠️ The unranked-forward case — the one thing that drops the suffix.** When the destination
  > sprint board is **unranked** (its Priority column is all `—`, no `P<n>` assigned to anything),
  > write the marker as `➡️ Moved to [Sprint N](sprint-N.md)` with **no `— priority M` suffix**: that
  > board is unranked, so there is no destination rank to name. This is the exact mirror of what
  > step 1 of the **reverse** move below says for this board, and it is stated here so the forward
  > rule is satisfiable in both cases rather than only the ranked one.
  > ⛔ **Never write `— priority —`, and never invent a number** — assigning a rank nobody ruled is
  > the act [ADR-035](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-035-a-mid-board-insertion-is-not-the-owner-ruled-re-rank-exception.md)
  > forbids, and it is worse than an absent suffix because it reads as a decision.
  > **⚠️ The omission is a deferral, not a permanent exemption: when the owner later ranks that board,
  > add every moved row's `— priority M` suffix in the same act.** The suffix is owed to a rank that
  > exists.
  >
  > **Worked precedent — [Sprint 6](sprint-6.md), 2026-08-14.** It opened unranked (the first board in
  > this project to do so) and its 18 rows here carried the bare marker, with the deviation declared
  > on that board rather than made silently. The owner ranked it `P1`–`P18` the same day, and the 18
  > `— priority P<n>` suffixes were added here in that act. **This clause exists because that board
  > had to deviate from a rule that no unranked destination could satisfy.**
  >
  > ⚠️ **Verified, not assumed — this is a documentation-truth rule, not a parser contract.**
  > `dashboard.sh`'s `moved_target` parser matches `Moved to \[*(<sprint-id>|Backlog)` and lets `.*`
  > swallow everything after the identity, so **it never reads the suffix**. Present, absent or
  > malformed, the suffix changes no count, no drift verdict and no `next` value. It is written for
  > the human reader, which is exactly why it must not carry an invented number.
- **Back on, from a sprint (the reverse move):** when the **producer** de-scopes a task out of a sprint
  and back onto this board, **five** edits, all mandatory:
  1. Flip the **sprint plan's** row to `➡️ Moved to [Backlog](backlog.md)` — the canonical marker from
     [`task-status-vocabulary.md`](../knowledge-base/conventions/task-status-vocabulary.md). **No
     `— priority M` suffix**: this board is unranked, so there is no destination rank to name. The href
     is **relative to the file holding the row** — `backlog.md` from `ai-agents/sprints/`, and it must
     become `../backlog.md` when that sprint plan is later archived under `sprints/done/`.
     **The sprint row is not deleted, and its own `P<n>` Priority cell is left alone** — a closed-out
     row is frozen history, and the number stays readable there.
  2. Add a row **here**, Status `🔲 Backlog`, Priority cell `—` (see *Priority*, below).
  3. **Update the brief's own `## Sprint` field to `Backlog`.**
  4. **Update the brief's own `## Status` field to `🔲 Backlog`** — a task de-scoped while it read
     `🔄 In progress` or `🚧 Blocked` is not in progress any more; it is unscheduled.
  5. **Update the brief's own `## Priority` field to `Unscheduled`** — the rank is *surrendered*, not
     parked. It stays recoverable by reading the sprint row it came from.
  > **⚠️ Skip edit 3 and the row never goes away** — same trap as **Off:** above, in the other
  > direction. Drift rule 2 compares the `Moved` target (`Backlog`) against the brief's `## Sprint`; a
  > mismatch is `drift disagreement`, and a drifted row always renders.
  >
  > **⚠️ Skip edit 4 and the NEW row here never goes away either** — the same trap, one field over.
  > This board cross-checks its own row against the brief's `## Status` (rule 1 is deliberately
  > disabled here), so a brief left reading `🔄 In progress` renders `waiting on owner` permanently.
  > Verified 2026-08-03 on a post-move fixture built by following these steps exactly:
  > `drift disagreement 0001 plan="🔲 Backlog" brief="🔄 In progress" brief_sprint="Backlog"
  > location="backlog/"`.
  >
  > **⚠️ Edit 5 is UNENFORCED — nothing in the repo checks it.** `dashboard.sh` reads a brief's
  > `Status`, `Sprint` and `Owner`; it never reads `## Priority`. A brief left reading `## Priority:
  > 152` on this unranked board is invisible to every control we have, so it survives only by being
  > done deliberately.
  >
  > **⚠️ Five here, three under Off:, and that is not an inconsistency.** The forward move needs no
  > `## Status` edit at all — a brief being pulled already reads `🔲 Backlog`, and so does the row it
  > gains in the sprint plan — and it folds its `## Priority` edit into its own step 3 as the
  > parenthetical *"(and give `## Priority` the real number)"* rather than numbering it.
- **Closed here:** a backlog task can be completed or cancelled without ever joining a sprint.
  `/fkit-task-done` / `/fkit-task-cancelled` already sweep `ai-agents/sprints/` recursively, so they
  find and flip rows in this file unchanged.

## Priority

**The Priority column reads `—` by design: this board is unranked.** Ranking is what a sprint is for.
A number here would be a commitment nobody made — and the briefs themselves record `## Priority:
Unscheduled` to match (some add a free-text qualifier after it, e.g.
`Unscheduled — high-value (…)`; that is the brief's own note and **never** becomes a number in this
column). If a task needs a rank, that is the signal to pull it into a sprint, not to number it here.

Must **score all FIVE triggers (a)–(e), not (e) alone**, taking the spec **as ADR-036 clause 4 states it** (clause 4 says of its own summary that it *"is a summary of it, not a second copy of it"* — **point at it, do not paraphrase**); classify each artifact into **exactly one of three named outcomes** — ownership-fact site / declared non-fact hit / **neither, a legitimate result** — with the reason; **state the registry noise count before and after, FRESHLY MEASURED** — ⚠️ the registry module is **authoritative** and **no count is hard-coded**, every figure in any report being a dated measurement of one tree on one day; any entry written honours the **≥30-char `reason` floor** (ADR-027 §Decision 3: *an entry with no stated reason is an unfalsifiable permanent hole*); run the completeness tripwire and report its result. ⛔ **Change NOTHING `0224` built** — not the log format, location, `deny()`, or the `**Role:**` line; a defect found there is **reported and filed, not fixed here**. ⛔ No registry building (`0189`), ⛔ ADR-036's triggers/thresholds/two-kinds **not reopened**, ⛔ no ADR amendment, ⛔ no new devDependency (ADR-014), ⛔ no `wiki-vault/` write (ADR-005), ⛔ no commit, no re-rank, no task-file move. **⚠️ NOT dependent on ADR-038 / `0222`** — `0224` is downstream of that ruling, but **this is downstream of `0224`'s ARTIFACTS, not of the ruling**: whatever ADR-038 says about which role runs which step, a new artifact naming roles and skills either belongs in the registry or does not, and that is **ADR-036's question, not ADR-038's**. **`Depends on: 0189, 0224`** — both open at filing; `Blocks: nothing`; owner: fkit-architect)* | [`0233-assess-the-denial-log-as-an-adr-036-registry-site`](../tasks/backlog/0233-assess-the-denial-log-as-an-adr-036-registry-site/brief.md) |

| ➡️ Moved to [Sprint 6](sprint-6.md) — priority P18 | — | **Replace the review coverage binary with ADR-042's three-state vocabulary** — *both reviewers measured* / *reasoning-only second opinion* / *Codex unavailable* *(**[ADR-042](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-042-a-codex-review-is-reasoning-only-and-reports-must-say-so.md) decision D1**, status **accepted** 2026-08-11, the **architect's call** — ⚠️ **the ADR is untracked in git at filing, which is expected, not a defect**; the degradation contract is a **binary** — Codex ran, or *"Codex reviewer unavailable"* — with **no vocabulary for the actual permanent state**: Codex ran, read the diff, reasoned well, and **measured nothing**, because `read-only` blocks `mkdtemp` so it can run neither the suite, nor a fixture, nor a mutation; three reviews this sprint on the **same flag and same capability** produced three different claims — `0259` *"Coverage is FULL"*, `0264` *"coverage is **not** partial"* (contradicting its own `:326-327`), `0265` **PARTIAL** and correct; **⛔ no report may claim FULL coverage on a reasoning-only Codex pass**; **⚠️ THE BINDING CONSTRAINT — this must land BEFORE OR WITH [`0273`](../tasks/backlog/0273-move-the-codex-review-sandbox-to-workspace-write-at-all-call-sites/brief.md), never after**, or D2 recreates the original defect in mirror image (claiming measurement because the flag permits it) — the edge is **structural**, `0273` carries `Depends on: 0272`; **⚠️ THE ONE DESIGN INSTRUCTION — write the rule in PER-RUN form from day one** (*claim "both measured" only on evidence in the Codex output that it executed something, never inferred from the flag*), so it needs **no re-edit** when `0273` lands **and none if `0273` is reverted** — ADR-042: *"D1 is unaffected by that revert and must stay in force either way"*; ⚠️ **the third state must NOT force the `🟡 Partial review` verdict** (ADR-042 D1 §3 — under today's sandbox that is *every* review, and treating the normal case as failure trains readers to ignore the banner that **does** signal failure) — it is a **coverage statement, not a verdict token**, and getting that balance right is the deliverable; tight form (matching only a table row's final Brief cell) → `total=297 no-row=2` (`0014`, `0004`)** — ⚠️ **the loose form reports a perfectly clean board while two tasks have no row**; ⛔ **and the tight `grep` is NOT the finished mechanism either** — it assumes the Brief cell is last on the line, so a re-wrapped row or a future added column silently drops out of its match: **whatever ships must parse the row, not pattern-match the line, or state that limit out loud.** ⚠️ **SECOND-ORDER LESSON, TO BE RECORDED AND ⛔ NOT SCOPED HERE:** `0295` was invisible for a day **because a change-surface report was taken on trust** — whether *that* wants a mechanism is **named for the owner**, not decided in this task. ⛔ **Scope EXCLUDES repairing `0014` and `0004`** — a repair before the decision destroys the only two live specimens and pre-empts the *"nothing changes"* candidate. ⛔ No mover ([ADR-033](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-033-task-movers-are-producer-only-reversing-adr-025.md) — the close goes through `/fkit-task-done`, producer-only, carrying the `(agent-closed — not owner-verified)` marker if the owner is absent), ⛔ no re-rank ([ADR-035](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-035-a-mid-board-insertion-is-not-the-owner-ruled-re-rank-exception.md)), ⛔ no commit. ⛔ **Filed by a spawned producer with no owner channel: UNRANKED, re-ranks nothing**; **recommended fix**: a **preflight guard** beside the existing checks at `:107-111`, before the test gate and before the first `writeFileSync`, that `fail()`s when `--branch <name>` is not the current HEAD branch — ✅ it fixes **both** runs, ✅ fails loud and early with the tree untouched, and ✅ **it does NOT require lifting `0288`'s `:271` fence**, which is the deciding argument; it had nowhere to live except a conversation**; **the five instances, ALL owner-ruled `left as-is`** — the vault CI count *"5 runs — 4 success, 1 failure"* (measured 2026-08-13; a 2026-08-14 measure found 9/7/1/1), `backlog.md`'s closed `0294` row (*"`select-active` measured today returns `active file="sprint-5.md"`"* + a settled `⚠️ OPEN QUESTION` block), its closed `0293` row (a condition since discharged), its closed `0297` row (*"⚠️ A SYNC IS CURRENTLY OWED"* — watermark now equals HEAD), and *"exactly **one** `sprint-*.md`"* — now **zero** — at `sprints/done/sprint-4.md:54`, `:105` and `sprint-5.md:24` (three separate rulings); `select-active` returns `active none`, **exit 3 being the documented no-active-sprint code, NOT a failure**); **⭐⭐ THE LOAD-BEARING FINDING — THIS IS NOT THE ADR-021 COLLISION IT LOOKS LIKE, re-verified on disk 2026-08-14**: the obvious objection is that a **spawned** worker has no `AskUserQuestion` so a spawned producer cannot collect consent ([ADR-021](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-021-askuserquestion-is-session-only-absent-in-consults.md)) — **it does not collide**, because `claude/skills/fkit-heal/SKILL.md`'s repair step 1 (*"Live session required"*) **already sanctions** the repair running in *"a `fkit producer` session, **or spawned under the sprint-loop relay pattern with every decision surfaced to the owner live**"*, and the lead **already owns** `/fkit-sprint-ship-loop` (verified in the `lead)` arm of `skills_for_role()`, `claude/skills-for-role.sh`) and already runs that relay pattern — so the lead can spawn a producer to run `check.sh` (**which never writes, in any branch**), relay `repair.sh propose` **verbatim**, hold the `AskUserQuestion` **itself**, and pass the approved `item` lines back to `repair.sh apply`: ⛔ **the mechanism is ALREADY AVAILABLE AND ALREADY LICENSED**, and any design proposing to invent a consent path, weaken ADR-021 or re-open ADR-039 **is answering the wrong question**; **⭐ THE ACTUAL GAP IS THE TRIGGER, AND ONLY THE TRIGGER** — `structure_notice()` in `claude/fkit-claude.sh` runs `check.sh`, filters rows against `ai-agents/.fkit-accepted-drift`, prints **one line to stderr** and is invoked once as `structure_notice \|\| :`; **that line goes to the terminal and the AGENT NEVER SEES IT** — not in the prompt, not in session state, nowhere the model reads; **⛔ THE TRIGGER MECHANISM IS THE QUESTION THIS TASK EXISTS TO ANSWER, NOT TO PICK OFF THE BRIEF** (candidates, non-exhaustive: launcher env var / launcher-dropped file / the lead running `check.sh` itself / a session-start prompt surface, each with its problem stated); **⚠️ TWO QUESTIONS UNDERNEATH IT ARE THE REAL REASON THIS NEEDS AN ARCHITECT, and neither is a coding decision** — **(i)** **may the lead execute a producer-owned skill's SCRIPTS directly?** `/fkit-heal` is the **producer's** in `skills_for_role()` (**the single declaration point**, sourced by **both** the launcher and the [ADR-018](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-018-pretooluse-skill-ownership-hook-replaces-consult-skills-exception-list.md) `PreToolUse` hook) — but that hook gates a **`Skill` call**, and `check.sh` is a **bash script**, so `bash .claude/skills/fkit-heal/check.sh` from the lead **would be denied by nothing**: ⛔ whether that is a legitimate read-only shortcut **or a hole in the ownership boundary** is exactly this task's kind of call ([ADR-010](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-010-role-locked-sessions-and-skill-lockdown.md), [ADR-012](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-012-skill-lockdown-is-session-scoped-frontmatter-dropped.md) — *a wall in a session, a rule in a consult*); **(ii)** **what surface makes the lead act at session start at all?** Nothing today instructs it to — answering means changing `claude/agents/fkit-lead.md`, or a skill, or adding one, i.e. **a change to a ROLE'S STANDING DEFINITION**, and ⚠️ whatever it is **fires on every lead launch**, including the overwhelming majority where nothing is repairable; **⚠️⚠️ THE REAL-WORLD CAVEAT THAT WILL MAKE A NAIVE DESIGN LOOK BROKEN** — in the owner's own project the diverging set includes **`CLAUDE.md`**, which projects customise heavily, so it will very likely classify **`owner-edited` → REPORT-ONLY WITH A DIFF, NOT REPAIRABLE**: `check.sh`'s own classification comment is the authority (*hash matches any `structure-manifest.tsv` row for the path → `untouched-stale`; matches nothing → `owner-edited`*), the manifest recording **every content hash fkit has ever shipped** per path — ⛔ **a design assuming "divergence ⇒ offer to fix" will, in the owner's own project, OFFER TO FIX NOTHING AND LOOK BROKEN**, because the notice reports *paths diverge* while only `untouched-stale` can be consented to, ⭐ and **getting that distinction into the design is arguably worth more than the trigger mechanism itself**; ⛔ **its hard consequence** — `SKILL.md` repair step 2: *"**Nothing eligible → say so and stop.** … **Never manufacture a consent question.**"* — **if every divergent path is `owner-edited` the lead must NOT ask, and a design that always asks has failed**; **⛔⛔ SIX ADR-039 CONSENT RULES MUST SURVIVE AND THE DESIGN MUST WALK THEM ITEM BY ITEM, NOT BLANKET-ASSURE** ([ADR-039](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-039-consent-gated-structure-repair-licensed-adr-015-invariant-unchanged.md), `SKILL.md` repair steps 3–7): **(1)** the proposal is shown **in full and verbatim** — every `item` line, every `# diff` block, every `# excluded:` line, because *"announcing a change **never** substitutes for consent … the proposal is the thing the owner approves, so they must see all of it"*; **(2)** **ONE** plan-level `AskUserQuestion` over that **exact enumerated list**, asked **with the diffs in view**, offering *approve the full list / approve a named subset / not now*; **(3)** apply **exactly** what was approved, *"verbatim and nothing else"*, behind an **apply-time freshness re-check** (`refused: changed-since-propose` — *"the consent given was to a diff that no longer exists"*), per-path output **verbatim, refusals and errors loudest**; **(4)** **consent is NEVER stored** — *"No file, no config, no env var, nothing that survives the run"*, no "always allow", no remembered approval, no progress state; **(5)** **nothing is ever moved, renamed or deleted** (v1 = replacement of untouched-stale fkit-authored files only); **(6)** **wiki-routed rows stay routed** — `repair.sh` *"refuses any `ai-agents/wiki-vault/` path outright"*, those repairs being `fkit-wiki`'s ([ADR-005](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-005-vendor-wiki-query-skill-reads-decentralized.md)); **(5)** ⭐ **the consent model — [ADR-039](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-039-consent-gated-structure-repair-licensed-adr-015-invariant-unchanged.md) §2 IS THE PRECEDENT AND MUST BE CITED** (owner ruling verbatim *"Plan-level approval (Recommended)"*: proposal enumerated with diffs in view, **one** plan-level `AskUserQuestion`, an **apply-time freshness re-check**, consent **never stored**) — the task must say whether commit consent **follows or deliberately departs** from that shape; **(6)** ⛔ **[ADR-024](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-024-ship-loop-owner-question-timeout-is-not-built.md) MUST NOT BE REOPENED** — that declined an auto-proceed **timeout** that REMOVED the owner from a gate, whereas ⭐ **this proposal ADDS one**, and the record must say so explicitly so a later reader cannot confuse them; **plus session scope**: `AskUserQuestion` is session-only ([ADR-021](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-021-askuserquestion-is-session-only-absent-in-consults.md)) so the gate can only ever be the **driver's**, and ⛔ **the loop's spawned workers keep their existing absolute no-commit ban, stated as explicit text rather than left inferable**; ⛔ **that bullet is specified for an ADR header and only an ADR has one** — an ADR opens H1 + a metadata bullet list (`- **Status:**` / `- **Date:**` / `- **Deciders:**` / `- **Scope:**`), whereas a **task brief** is H1 + heading-and-value sections (`## ID` / `## Sprint` / `## Priority` / `## Status` / `## Owner` / `## Context` / `## What to build` / `## Verification steps` / `## Notes`) with **no metadata bullet list anywhere**, and a **board row** is one cell of a four-column table with **no header at all** — so corrections written into briefs and rows ship **without** the warning the form relies on; ⛔⛔ **THIS TASK FRAMES THE DECISION AND GETS IT RULED — IT DOES NOT ARRIVE HAVING PICKED ONE**; **two options, neither pre-decided**: **(A)** define a header-warning equivalent for briefs **and separately for rows**, or **(B)** record deliberately that they get none and **standardise one of the four substitutes instead**, stating plainly what is lost; and its dependency parser is a closed four-form grammar whose own **rule 3** says *"BI is the ONLY form with an unambiguous in-band terminator. For BL/P/S, **OVER-INCLUDE** trailing prose rather than guess where the dependency ends"* — so a `- **Corrections:**` bullet written next to `- **Depends on:**` can be **swallowed into the dependency text and rendered into the board's Task cell**, the task-84 family recorded in [`dependency-declaration-form.md`](../knowledge-base/conventions/dependency-declaration-form.md); ⛔ **a parser change is its own brief, not this one's**; ⚠️⚠️ **RELATIONSHIP TO [`0311`](../tasks/backlog/0311-specify-the-corrections-bullet-own-date-and-metadata-position/brief.md) — STATED, DELIBERATELY NOT RESOLVED**: `0311` covers the `- **Corrections:**` bullet's own date and metadata position **for ADRs**; **provenance**: surfaced by [`0302`](../tasks/done/0302-pressing-enter-at-the-role-menu-should-open-the-lead/brief.md)'s post-review re-verification the same day; ⭐ **THE GAP, re-verified firsthand on disk by the filing producer 2026-08-21 and not carried on anyone's word**: a stateful review reviews an **uncommitted working tree**, and the reviewed state is **never captured anywhere** — `claude/skills/fkit-stateful-review/SKILL.md` and `claude/skills/fkit-process-stateful-review/SKILL.md` carry the **same** three-field ledger header (`Task:` / `File(s) under review: <paths>` / `Status:`) and `File(s) under review` is specified as **paths**, the *names* of files and never their contents or a digest, with a grep of both files for `stash`/`blob`/`sha`/`hash`/`baseline`/`snapshot`/`checksum` returning **no hit on any of those words** (every line returned matched only the substring `share` inside *"shared"*); so when a later pass asks *"did any executable line change since the review?"* it **cannot byte-compare** and can only **infer**; ⚠️ **what a live header does carry, read from `0302`'s `review.md` this run (⛔ read as evidence, NOT edited — other workers held that folder)**: hand-written diff counts in prose (*"now +8/−2, uncommitted"*, *"round 1 reviewed +7/−2"*) — ⭐ **a count is not an identity**, many edits produce `+7/−2` — plus an **unprescribed** round line *"Baseline `HEAD = 7832cba`"* which identifies the **committed base, not the working tree**, and the uncommitted delta on top of `HEAD` is exactly what was reviewed; **how the claim was actually established there**: the reviewer *"Stripped every whole-line comment from `HEAD:claude/fkit-claude.sh` and from the working tree, then diffed the remainder"* finding *"exactly the round-1 change, and nothing else"*, plus a `--numstat` delta argument (`8 2` against round 1's `+7/−2` ⇒ one added comment line) — **sound, and still inference**, and it held only because the change was one tracked file with a two-line executable delta fully reproducible from `HEAD`; ⚠️ **CORRECTION TO THE ROUTING NOTE THAT FILED THIS ROW**: it described **four separately-enumerated convergent checks**; the ledger actually records **two** convergent arguments (the comment-stripped `HEAD`↔worktree diff, and the `--numstat` delta) — same substance, same conclusion, different shape, and the gap is unchanged because **every one of them compares against `HEAD` or a remembered count, never against the reviewed state**; ⛔⛔ **THIS TASK FRAMES THE DECISION AND GETS IT RULED — IT DOES NOT ARRIVE HAVING PICKED ONE**, and in particular ⛔ **the suggested `git stash create` blob id does NOT do what it appears to** — **measured firsthand in a scratch repo, git 2.50.1 (Apple Git-155)**: `git stash create` leaves the worktree and refs untouched but its snapshot tree contains **tracked modifications ONLY** (`git ls-tree -r <obj>` omits the untracked file), and the obvious fix `git stash create -u` **silently swallows `-u` as the COMMIT MESSAGE** (the object's message reads literally `On main: -u`) leaving the untracked file absent from the snapshot **and** from both parents — `create` takes a `<message>`, not flags, and `git stash create -h` likewise returns a hash instead of help; **four options, none pre-decided**: **(A)** a `git stash create` object id in the header — ⛔ tracked-only per the probe, ⚠️ durability under `git gc` **UNMEASURED and must be priced before A is put to the owner**; **(B)** per-file digests of the files under review (`git hash-object`, which **does** digest untracked files — proven in the same probe) — needs a rule for what counts as "the files under review" when the set grows between rounds; **(C)** require the reviewed state be committed first — ⚠️ **collides with the universal hard rule *"never commit or push unless the owner explicitly asks"***, so it is an owner gate mid-run and overlaps [`0310`](../tasks/backlog/0310-gate-the-sprint-ship-loop-on-an-owner-approved-commit-of-implemented-work/brief.md); **(D)** accept convergent inference and document the **reasoning standard** instead — costs nothing at review time, but must say where inference stops being admissible; ⚠️ **CONSTRAINTS ANY ANSWER MUST RESPECT**: fkit never commits unprompted; the sprint-run working tree carries **many tasks' concurrent in-flight work** (`0302`'s own header lists `0145`'s brief under *"In the same working tree but NOT part of `0302`'s diff"*) so a whole-tree snapshot may identify but any comparison must be scoped to **this task's** files; and task-folder ledgers are themselves **untracked mid-run**, which is [`0313`](../tasks/backlog/0313-decide-the-append-only-proof-standard-for-untracked-task-folder-ledgers/brief.md)'s territory; ⚠️⚠️ **RELATIONSHIP TO `0313` — STATED, DELIBERATELY NOT RESOLVED**: `0313` is *"decide the append-only proof standard for untracked task-folder ledgers"* — **proving how a ledger changed**; this is **proving what state was reviewed** — adjacent, same family, **different question** (its subject is the ledger document, this one's is the code under review); ⛔ **the owner considered folding them on 2026-08-21 and chose a separate task — the filing producer did NOT fold them and must not**; ⚠️ they do interact (both turn on git's untracked-file behaviour) so **whoever runs second reads the other's ruling first**; ⛔ **phase 2 edits BOTH review skills or NEITHER** — they carry the schema block verbatim and each says *"keep it exact so the two sides interoperate"*; ⛔ **do not edit `0302`'s folder** (two workers active there at filing time — it is read evidence, not a work surface); ⛔ edit the canonical `claude/` source **never** the gitignored `.claude/` copy, no commit, no push, no wiki write ([ADR-005](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-005-vendor-wiki-query-skill-reads-decentralized.md)); ⚠️ **citations are file + quoted phrase, never `:NNN`** — living documents, the `0143` residual; ⚠️ **filed UNRANKED by a SPAWNED producer with no owner channel — this row APPENDS and renumbers nothing, and was deliberately NOT added to Sprint 6** ([ADR-035](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-035-a-mid-board-insertion-is-not-the-owner-ruled-re-rank-exception.md), [ADR-021](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-021-askuserquestion-is-session-only-absent-in-consults.md)); | ➡️ Moved to [Sprint 6](sprint-6.md) — priority P13 | — | **Add a self-locator repair rule to `/fkit-task-done` — repair the moved folder's OWN header locators at every close** *(**owner-ruled 2026-08-23**, live via `AskUserQuestion` in an `fkit lead` session driving `/fkit-sprint-ship-loop` and relayed to a spawned producer — **verbatim option label: "Re-point them, add rule to task-done (Recommended)"**; arose from `0250`'s close-out `NEEDS-DECISION`. **The gap, verified on disk 2026-08-23:** step 5 of `claude/skills/fkit-task-done/SKILL.md` mandates inbound link repair and outbound **sibling** link repair — its own words scope it to *"a link **it** … makes to a **sibling** task"* — and it is **silent on the folder pointing at itself**, so `plan.md`'s `Brief:` line and `review.md`'s `Task:` line die at every close, **2 new instances per close, forever**. ⭐ **THE LOAD-BEARING DISTINCTION, and the rule is actively harmful without it: a locator is not a claim.** A header line saying *where the brief is* is a pointer, and a dead pointer is simply wrong — **repair it**. A **captured command output**, a **quoted specimen**, or a **dated measurement** that mentions the old path is **frozen evidence** — **rewriting it falsifies a dated record**. ⛔ **A rule implemented as "replace `backlog/` with `done/` in the moved folder" WOULD CORRUPT the freeze cases** — it must key on the locator's ROLE, never on the string. **Worked example — `0250`'s folder, 6 occurrences splitting 2 repair / 4 freeze**: repair `plan.md:5` and `review.md:3` (**already executed 2026-08-23** under the ruling's first half, one changed line per file, the four freezes left byte-identical); freeze `plan.md:117` (captured `git status --porcelain` inside a fence), `plan.md:188` (frozen plan step as written at plan time), `review.md:86` and `review.md:98` (**dated measurements about `0324` that say `backlog/` CORRECTLY — rewriting them makes them FALSE**). **Measured firsthand 2026-08-23:** `ai-agents/tasks/done/` holds **198** task folders, of which **44** `review.md`, **1** `plan.md` (`0248-update-the-docs-for-the-structure-check-capability`) and **1** `worklog.md` (`0218-repair-0177s-stale-cap-and-byte-figures`) carry a stale self-locator; ⚠️ **these are FLOORS, not totals** — the check read only each file's first 12 lines for the literal full folder name, so it is blind to elided or relative forms and to locators further down. **The class is GROWING:** `0168` measured 124 folders / 60 `review.md` on 2026-07-31; today it is 198 / 116. ⭐ **DISTINCT FROM `0168` — MEASURED, NOT GUESSED; file it, do not fold it**: `0168` is **retroactive** and **`review.md`-only** (its 44 existing instances), this is **prospective** and edits **the mover**, covering `plan.md` / `review.md` / `worklog.md` — **and the proof they do not overlap is that `0248`'s `plan.md` and `0218`'s `worklog.md` are stale TODAY and sit outside `0168`'s own stated scope**. **REPRODUCED FIRSTHAND BY THE FILING PRODUCER 2026-08-23** against a clean `git archive HEAD claude` share (extracted script `shasum` `a8e00a69effda2fd745f2e050d10ce14af61dde1`) in scratch dirs outside the repo, **all three shapes exit `1`**: `.fkit` a **dangling symlink** (`mkdir: …/.fkit: No such file or directory`, no `.gitignore` written), `.fkit` a **regular file** (`File exists`, same), `.claude` a **regular file** (`Not a directory`, **dies even earlier — no agents, no skills, no gitignore**); ⚠️ **`0046` FIXES THE SYMLINK SHAPE OF THE `.fkit` ABORT, NOT THE GENERAL SHAPE — ⛔ DO NOT CLOSE THIS AS ALREADY-DONE ONCE `0046` LANDS**, since shapes 2 and 3 contain no symlink at all and shape 3 is not even `0046`'s section; ⛔⛔ **ONE PREMISE IN THE RELAY WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE RATHER THAN CARRIED**: the finding reached this producer claiming *"the user silently loses setup on every launch and is never told"* — **that half is FALSE, disproved by EXECUTION not by reading**, because running `claude/fkit-claude.sh coder` with a stubbed `claude` binary on a dangling-`.fkit` project printed a loud three-line `⚠ fkit could not finish setting up this project.` warning; **the launcher's `0088` guard WORKS** (`:355-361` captures `setup_rc`, `:373-377` warns on any non-zero other than `3`), ⭐ **the user IS told**; two further measured corrections — **"starts the session anyway" is not always true** (in the `.claude`-regular-file shape no agent reaches disk and `:387-392` **refuses to launch and exits 1**, deliberately, so an unroled session cannot fail open past the ADR-010 lockdown), and 🆕 **a NEW adjacent finding not in the original audit: with a dangling `.fkit` the launcher MISDIAGNOSES the cause**, `:336` printing `⚠ <proj> is not writable …` **on a fully writable project** — ✅ **the lockdown still applied inline so it failed SAFE not open**, and ⚠️ **that line is in the launcher not init, so it is carried on `0330`, recorded here only because it is what a reader of this task will SEE on screen and otherwise misread**; ⭐ **SO THE DEFECT AND THE DAMAGE ARE REAL BUT SILENT-AND-UNTOLD IS NOT THE REASON — state it as "a recoverable, announced-elsewhere condition takes down three later sections that had nothing to do with it, against a bar the rest of the file meets", and ⛔ do not restate the silent framing**; **WHAT TO BUILD** — make both calls non-fatal, warn with the **real cause** naming the path (⛔ **not a bare `mkdir:` line leaking from the shell, which is what users get today**), **skip cleanly rather than half-write** (no `cat >`/`chmod +x` into a `.fkit` that does not exist, no `cp` into a missing dir), and ⭐ **decide and STATE what init's exit status becomes — the load-bearing decision, not a detail**, since `claude/fkit-claude.sh:363-367` reads it as a three-way signal (`0` success, `3` set-up-but-refused-`ai-agents/`, anything else a real failure) whose comment warns *"the two copies drifted the first time we tried"* re-testing in the launcher, so **choosing `0` silences a warning the user currently gets and choosing non-zero keeps it but must not brick the launch**; **VERIFICATION** — all three measured shapes asserted separately reaching the end with `.gitignore` written, §6 run and the summary printed; the warning text asserted; the launcher contract re-checked per shape **including the refuse-to-launch case, which should survive**; the `ai-agents/`-refused path still returns `3`; the ordinary case still exits `0`; `npm test` green with the count stated; ⚠️ **check whether `test/prove-red.sh` reaches this file before claiming mutation coverage — `0046`'s plan records its only reference is a COMMENT at `:12`, so a new guard here is plausibly UNMUTATED, and if so SAY SO plainly rather than letting it pass unremarked** (`0037` is the open task for that seam); **depends on nothing**; ⚠️ **overlaps `0327` at exactly ONE line, `:485`, treated for different reasons — `0327` stops it traversing a symlink, this stops it killing init; ⛔ whichever lands second must not assume the other covered its case, and both touching `:485` is a MERGE-ORDER NOTE not a dependency**; ⛔ **distinct from `0045`, measured not assumed** — that is the **read** side under **`ai-agents/`** and **latent** by its own brief's words, this is a **write-path abort** under `.fkit`/`.claude`, live today; **neither closes the other**; **adjacent to `0088`**, closing the gap on init's own side of the bar `0088` set on the launcher's side, **without re-opening it**; **relates to `0329`** and **`0330`**; ⛔ **OUT OF SCOPE** — `0327`, `0329`, `0330`, `0046`'s §4 fix, extending `prove-red.sh` (`0037`), any `ai-agents/wiki-vault/` write ([ADR-005](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-005-vendor-wiki-query-skill-reads-decentralized.md)), any re-rank ([ADR-035](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-035-a-mid-board-insertion-is-not-the-owner-ruled-re-rank-exception.md)) and any task-file move ([ADR-033](../knowledge-base/decisions/adr-033-task-movers-are-producer-only-reversing-adr-025.md)); ⚠️ **the symlink still points OUT of the project, so the escape is real — these two properties BOUND the damage, they do not make it in-project**; **THE DECISION, BOTH OPTIONS WITH HONEST COSTS AND ⛔ NEITHER RECOMMENDED BY THIS BRIEF** — **(a) REFUSE, like every other site**: ✅ consistency, no fkit write anywhere goes through a symlink and the file's own doctrine comment (~`:159-172`, *"`[ -L ]` FIRST, ALWAYS … -L is the one test that does not lie"*) becomes exceptionless, one rule to explain and to test; **(b) ALLOW IT DELIBERATELY AND RECORD WHY IN THE CODE**: ✅ respects a real workflow with the two measured properties as the argument, **zero behaviour change and zero regression risk**; ⛔ leaves one site inconsistent with a doctrine whose value is being exceptionless — an exception is a thing every future reader and every future audit must re-learn — and ⛔ **it is still an out-of-project write, the comment making it INTENDED not CONTAINED**; ⚠️ **the comment is then the ENTIRE deliverable so it must be strong enough to stop the next audit re-filing this**; ⚠️ **A THIRD SHAPE EXISTS AND THE PLAN SHOULD NAME IT RATHER THAN LET IT BE DISCOVERED LATE — allow-but-announce**, following the link and printing one line saying fkit wrote outside the project; ## Addendum — the owner-ruled fold of 2026-08-11 (`0271` grows from three items to five)

**Authority:** owner ruling 2026-08-11, given live via `AskUserQuestion` in a `fkit lead` session,
verbatim option label **"Fold into 0271"**. Source of truth for the wording is
[`0265`](../tasks/done/0265-implement-adr-041s-dashboard-half-the-backlog-identity-token-and-the-resolve-identity-interface/brief.md)'s
`review.md`, *Accepted residuals*.

Two residuals from `0265`'s review were folded into the existing `0271` brief rather than filed as a
new task. They are the same shape as its first three items — **a correct behavior with no test that
reds when it is undone** — and cover `0265`'s `select-active` half:

| Added item | Was | What is unpinned |
|---|---|---|
| **4** | `0265` R4 / residual **A1** | The `[ -f ]` no-match guard on the candidate glob — **removing it leaves the suite green (141/141)**. When it fires (empty `sprints/`) the result is a phantom candidate record and a `head:` stderr line; **selection stays correct**. ⚠️ The *dangerous* half, the `set +f` wrapper, **is already pinned** — removing it reds **7** tests. Do not conflate them. |
| **5** | `0265` R7 / residual **A2** | Two comment claims no test exercises: (a) ADR-041 §1.1's `sprints/done/` exclusion, and (b) *"a plan file literally named `identity` still renders as a board"*. Both verified correct by direct probe in `0265`; neither reds if undone. |

⛔ **Ruled OUT of the fold, and not to be pulled in later without a new ruling:** `0265`'s **R1** (false
locale guard) and **R5** (unreadable candidate resolving to a wrong identity) — both **fixed and
red-proved inside `0265`**, and R1 additionally recorded by the reviewer as outside `0271`'s scope;
`0265` residual **A3/R6** (leading-dash path noise — **accepted as-is**, fails safe); and residual
**A4** (newline/TAB in a basename — **accepted as-is, code unchanged**, now disclosed as a limit).
**The task grew by exactly two items.**

⚠️ **The folder name still says "three"** —
`0271-pin-the-three-unpinned-behaviors-in-the-sprint-identity-grammar`. Renaming it is a task-file
move, which only the mover skills may perform, so the in-file H1 was updated and the divergence
flagged in the brief instead. **The folder name is dated, not wrong.**

Recorded by a spawned `fkit-producer` with no owner channel, on the owner's ruling of the same day.
